Here\u2019s Who Else Wins. By Jeremy Phillips Published Apr 16, 7:35AM EDT Follow 24/7 Wall St. on Google This post may contain links from our sponsors and affiliates, and Flywheel Publishing may receive compensation for actions taken through them. \u200b How to Add Us to Google News Sending You to Google News in 3 \u00a9 Gorodenkoff / Shutterstock.com CoreWeave ( NASDAQ:CRWV ) just secured a $21 billion commitment from Meta, expanding what was already a landmark relationship and cementing CoreWeave as the preferred AI cloud infrastructure partner for the world\u2019s largest social platform. We\u2019ve been watching CoreWeave for the past year now, and this deal is the kind of contract that rewrites a company\u2019s story. For context: CoreWeave had already announced a multi-year deal with Meta worth up to $14.2B in Q3 2025 . This expansion pushes the total commitment to $21 billion, reflecting how aggressively Meta Platforms ( NASDAQ:META | META Price Prediction ) is scaling its AI infrastructure. Meta guided for $115 to $135 billion in capital expenditures in 2026 , and its Meta Superintelligence Labs initiative is driving enormous compute demand that its own data centers cannot fully absorb. CoreWeave\u2019s stock has responded. We\u2019re seeing shares up roughly 34% over the past week, trading at $118.69 against an IPO filing price of $89.05. The company already carries a $66.8 billion revenue backlog, and full-year 2025 revenue hit $5.13 billion, up 168% year over year. The clearest secondary winner here is NVIDIA Corporation ( NASDAQ:NVDA ). CoreWeave runs on NVIDIA GPUs, holds a $6.3 billion strategic collaboration with NVIDIA, and was named NVIDIA Exemplar Cloud for training workloads on the GB200 NVL72. Every dollar Meta commits to CoreWeave flows through NVIDIA\u2019s hardware stack. If you believe Meta\u2019s AI ambitions are real and durable, CoreWeave is the toll road you have to pay attention to, and NVIDIA is the company that paved it. CoreWeave Announces Intention to Offer $1,000 million of 9.750% Senior Notes due 2031 Business Wire Thu, 16 April 2026 at 4:15 am GMT-7 3 min read CRWV LIVINGSTON, N.J., April 16, 2026 --( BUSINESS WIRE )--CoreWeave, Inc. (Nasdaq: CRWV ) (\"CoreWeave\") announced today that it intends, subject to market and other customary conditions, to offer $1,000 million aggregate principal amount of 9.750% senior notes due 2031 (the \"Notes\") in a private offering. The Notes will be guaranteed on a senior unsecured basis by certain wholly-owned subsidiaries of CoreWeave. The Notes are being offered as additional notes under the Indenture, dated as of April 14, 2026, pursuant to which CoreWeave previously issued $1,750 million in aggregate principal amount of 9.750% senior notes due 2031. CoreWeave intends to use the proceeds from the offering of the Notes for general corporate purposes, including, without limitation, repayment of outstanding indebtedness, and to pay fees, costs and expenses in connection with the offering of the Notes. The Notes and related guarantees are being offered only to persons reasonably believed to be qualified institutional buyers in reliance on Rule 144A under the Securities Act of 1933, as amended (the \"Securities Act\"), or to persons other than \"U.S. persons\" in compliance with Regulation S under the Securities Act. The Notes and related guarantees have not been and will not be registered under the Securities Act or the securities laws of any other jurisdiction and may not be offered or sold in the United States except pursuant to an exemption from, or in a transaction not subject to, the registration requirements of the Securities Act. This press release is for informational purposes only and is not an offer to sell or the solicitation of an offer to buy any securities, nor shall there be any sale of securities in any state or jurisdiction in which such offer, solicitation or sale would be unlawful prior to registration or qualification under the securities laws of any such state or jurisdiction. This is CoreWeave\u2019s third large April agreement, following earlier deals with Meta Platforms and Anthropic. The partnership broadens CoreWeave\u2019s reach into quantitative finance and supports further build out of Nvidia powered data centers. CoreWeave, traded as NasdaqGS:CRWV, sits at the center of high performance infrastructure for large scale AI workloads, and the Jane Street deal adds another large customer to that story. The stock last closed at $117.2, with a 7 day return of 37.5%, a 30 day return of 44.5%, and a 1 year return of 187.4%. Those moves reflect growing attention on the company\u2019s role as a specialist AI cloud provider rather than a broad based hyperscaler. For you as an investor, the Jane Street commitment highlights how demand for CoreWeave\u2019s infrastructure now extends beyond AI developers into capital markets and quantitative research. The deal also comes alongside earlier April agreements with Meta Platforms and Anthropic. Together, these point to a wider customer mix and additional support for CoreWeave\u2019s Nvidia powered build out, though the financial impact will depend on execution and how these multi year commitments translate into actual usage over time.     "published": "2026-04-15T17:17:00Z",
    "content": "Jane Street signs $6 billion AI cloud deal with CoreWeave, invests $1 billion in equity Skip to content Toggle Navigation News Events TNW Conference June 19 & 20, 2025 All events Spaces Programs Newsletters Partner with us Jobs Contact News Latest Deep tech Sustainability Ecosystems Data and security Fintech and ecommerce Future of work Conference media hub More Startups and technology Investors and funding Government and policy Corporates and innovation Podcast Artificial Intelligence Jane Street commits $6 billion to CoreWeave and takes a $1 billion equity stake April 15, 2026 - 5:17 pm In short: Jane Street has signed a $6 billion AI cloud agreement with CoreWeave and taken a $1 billion equity stake at $109 per share, making the quantitative trading firm one of CoreWeave\u2019s five largest shareholders. The deal provides Jane Street with access to NVIDIA\u2019s next-generation Vera Rubin compute and adds to CoreWeave\u2019s growing contract book, which includes Meta ($35B), OpenAI ($12B), and NVIDIA ($6.3B in capacity commitments). Jane Street, the quantitative trading firm that generated $20.5 billion in net trading revenue last year, has signed a $6 billion AI cloud agreement with CoreWeave and taken a $1 billion equity stake in the company, a deal that says as much about the future of finance as it does about the AI infrastructure market. Under the agreement, CoreWeave will provide Jane Street with access to next-generation compute across multiple data centre facilities, including systems built on NVIDIA\u2019s forthcoming Vera Rubin architecture. Jane Street\u2019s $1 billion equity investment, at $109 per share, makes it one of CoreWeave\u2019s five largest shareholders and values the cloud provider\u2019s stock at a 176% premium to its IPO price just thirteen months ago. Why a trading firm needs $6 billion in cloud compute Jane Street is not a typical CoreWeave customer. The firm, founded in 2000 with offices in New York, London, Hong Kong, Singapore, and Amsterdam, runs a research-driven trading operation that already deploys tens of thousands of high-end GPUs across its own computing infrastructure. Its engineers build neural network models that power trading strategies across global financial markets, processing massive volumes of noisy market data in real time. The \ud83d\udc9c of EU tech The latest rumblings from the EU tech scene, a story from our wise ol' founder Boris, and some questionable AI art. It's free, every week, in your inbox. Sign up now! The firm\u2019s statement framed the deal in language usually reserved for AI research labs: \u201c training large, complex models on massive volumes of noisy data, refining them continuously, and deploying at a scale to help make markets more efficient. \u201d Max Hjelm, CoreWeave\u2019s senior vice president of revenue, was more direct: \u201c Jane Street operates like a frontier lab. \u201d That comparison is not hyperbole. Jane Street\u2019s 2024 net income was $13 billion, roughly what it costs to train a frontier language model several times over. Through the first three quarters of 2025, the firm\u2019s revenue had already exceeded $24 billion, with a single quarter, Q2 2025, producing"

    "content": "Jane Street commits $7 billion to CoreWeave in cloud and equity deal Search Newsletters Editions Home Latest Business News Money & Markets Tech & Innovation A.I. Lifestyle Leadership \u2709\ufe0f Emails \ud83c\udfa7 Podcasts Markets Jane Street is committing $6 billion to CoreWeave's AI cloud and buying $1 billion in stock The trading firm will spend $6 billion on CoreWeave's AI cloud platform and has purchased $1 billion in CoreWeave stock at $109 a share By Cris Tolomia Share to X Share to Facebook Share to Reddit Share to Email Share to Link Share to X Share to Facebook Share to Reddit Share to Email Share to Link Add Quartz on Google NurPhoto / Getty Images Jane Street has committed about $6 billion to use CoreWeave's AI cloud platform and made a separate $1 billion equity investment in the company, bringing its total commitment to $7 billion, the companies said April 15. Under the cloud agreement, CoreWeave will give Jane Street access to compute across multiple data center facilities, including Nvidia $NVDA 's Vera Rubin technology, along with the software and services needed to deploy and scale its AI operations. CoreWeave said the arrangement includes dedicated connectivity, custom storage configurations, and technical support tailored to Jane Street's research needs. Related Content Morgan Stanley posted record quarterly revenue as trading and M&A fees surged Bank of America posted a 17% profit jump in Q1 as trading and interest income surged According to Reuters , the $109-per-share price Jane Street paid for its CoreWeave equity stake represented a 7% discount to where the stock last closed. Reuters reported that with the new purchase folded in, Jane Street holds roughly $1.44 billion worth of CoreWeave stock in total, a position large enough to rank it among the company's five biggest shareholders. \"We are deeply committed to investing in cutting-edge technologies that support our research in global financial markets, training large, complex models on massive volumes of noisy data, refining them continuously, and deploying at a scale to help make markets more efficient,\" Jane Street said in a statement. \"Access to CoreWeave's leading AI cloud platform enables our researchers to move at the pace our competitive business demands.\" Max Hjelm, Senior Vice President of Revenue at CoreWeave, said in a statement that \"Jane Street operates like a frontier lab, continually breaking new ground in deep learning and pushing the scale and complexity of their models.\" The deal is the third major agreement CoreWeave has announced this month. Earlier, CoreWeave signed a $21 billion agreement with Meta $META to supply AI cloud capacity through 2032, expanding a prior contract valued at about $14 billion. A day later, CoreWeave reached a multi-year agreement with Anthropic to provide compute supporting the development and deployment of Claude AI models, though financial terms of that deal were not disclosed. CoreWeave, which completed its public listing on Nasdaq $NDAQ in March 2025, has been working to broaden its customer base and expand its software offerings beyond access to Nvidia processors.     "content": "Tens of billions in days: CoreWeave shows how aggressively AI infrastructure is being funded | Fortune Search Subscribe Home Latest Fortune 500 Finance Tech Leadership Lifestyle Rankings Multimedia Newsletters CFO Daily Tens of billions in days: CoreWeave shows how aggressively AI infrastructure is being funded By Sheryl Estrada Sheryl Estrada Senior Writer and author of CFO Daily Down Arrow Button Icon By Sheryl Estrada Sheryl Estrada Senior Writer and author of CFO Daily Down Arrow Button Icon April 13, 2026, 6:55 AM ET Add us on Getty Images Good morning. For finance leaders trying to understand how the AI infrastructure race is being funded, CoreWeave just offered a revealing case study. Recommended Video In the span of a few days, the Nvidia-backed startup locked in tens of billions in customer commitments and layered on multiple forms of debt. This underscores a defining feature of today\u2019s AI boom: growth is being financed as aggressively as it is being engineered. CoreWeave, which provides cloud-based GPUs powered by Nvidia chips, announced on Thursday a $21 billion deal with Meta running from 2027 to 2032. That brings Meta\u2019s total commitment to more than $35 billion, which could be viewed as a significant vote of confidence in sustained demand for AI compute. At the same time, the company raised roughly $3.5 billion in convertible senior notes, a hybrid instrument that blends debt with an equity upside. Investors collect interest, but can convert into shares if CoreWeave\u2019s valuation rises. It\u2019s a structure that limits near-term cash strain while effectively betting that future equity will be worth more. And that wasn\u2019t all. CoreWeave CFO Nitin Agrawal said in a LinkedIn post on Friday that the company also: \u2014Upsized a high-yield bond offering due to heavy demand. \u2014Secured an $8.5 billion delayed draw term loan at investment-grade ratings. \u2014Executed what it described as one of the largest dual-tranche raises of its kind. Also on Friday, the company\u2019s stock climbed as much as 13% after it announced a multi-year agreement with Anthropic . Taken together, it shows that capital is flowing freely to AI infrastructure\u2014at least for now. I asked Morningstar equity analyst Luke Yang what this shows for how AI-native companies are approaching capital formation right now. He pointed to three aspects: \u2014Cloud infrastructure companies will continue to use all kinds of tools available to get the funds necessary for capacity expansion. \u201cFor this Meta-CoreWeave deal specifically, we see the company leveraging delayed draw term loans, corporate bonds, and convertible notes. Neoclouds also use OEM financing, operating/financing leases, share issuance, etc. Growth is the top priority, and financing should not be a bottleneck for these companies\u2019 growth.\u201d \u2014Creditors are getting more comfortable with the business of neoclouds. \u201cWe see a sequential improvement in the interest rates and credit ratings of CoreWeave\u2019s delayed draw term loans. The DDTL 4.0 facility is the first investment-grade facility with around a 6% interest rate. Going forward, we expect to see more neocloud companies securing investment-grade financing with take-or-pay deals from credible AI"

    "content": "CoreWeave: $6 Billion AI Cloud Agreement And $1 Billion Equity Investment From Jane Street VC PE M&A Asset Management Law Firms Profiles Contact \u00d7 Search Pulse 2.0 CoreWeave: $6 Billion AI Cloud Agreement And $1 Billion Equity Investment From Jane Street By Amit Chowdhry \u2022 Yesterday at 12:05 PM CoreWeave: $6 Billion AI Cloud Agreement And $1 Billion Equity Investment From Jane Street CoreWeave announced a $6 billion AI cloud agreement with Jane Street, alongside a $1 billion equity investment, significantly expanding their partnership. Under the agreement, CoreWeave will provide Jane Street with access to next-generation compute infrastructure across multiple facilities, including systems powered by advanced NVIDIA Vera Rubin technology, as well as integrated software and services to support the deployment and scaling of AI models. In addition to the cloud commitment, Jane Street has invested $1 billion in CoreWeave Class A common stock at $109.00 per share, reinforcing its long-term commitment to leveraging high-performance computing and machine learning across its trading and research operations. The expanded partnership reflects increasing demand for large-scale AI infrastructure within quantitative trading, where firms rely on complex models trained on vast datasets to optimize market strategies and execution. CoreWeave\u2019s platform is designed to support these requirements through high-performance computing, custom storage configurations, dedicated connectivity, and integrated software systems that enable efficient and scalable AI workloads in production environments. Founded in 2000, Jane Street operates globally with a research-driven approach to trading, while CoreWeave continues to position itself as a leading provider of AI-focused cloud infrastructure following its 2025 public listing. The agreement underscores the growing convergence of advanced AI capabilities and financial markets, as firms invest heavily in infrastructure to maintain competitive advantages in increasingly data-driven environments. KEY QUOTES: \u201cWe are deeply committed to investing in cutting-edge technologies that support our research in global financial markets, training large, complex models on massive volumes of noisy data, refining them continuously, and deploying at a scale to help make markets more efficient.     "content": "AI Mega-Investments Surge as Anthropic Eyes $800B Valuation Company Home US Stock Blog News Insider Market Outlook Join us Support FAQ Legal central Press Country Global Indonesia US Stock Learning Hub \ud83c\udf0f Global Get the app Home News AI Mega-Investments Surge as Anthropic Eyes $800B Valuation AI Mega-Investments Surge as Anthropic Eyes $800B Valuation Rendy Andriyanto Gotrade Team Apr 15, 2026 Reviewed by Gotrade Internal Analyst Share this article Gotrade News - The AI investment race is reaching a new level of intensity this week as three landmark deals signal that institutional capital is flooding into artificial intelligence infrastructure at an unprecedented pace. Anthropic is fielding offers that could push its valuation to $800 billion, CoreWeave just locked in a $6 billion cloud agreement with Jane Street, and Broadcom (AVGO) expanded a multi-year custom chip partnership with Meta (META) . These deals collectively reinforce that the AI buildout is accelerating rather than peaking, with capital commitments now stretching years into the future. For global investors, the message is clear: the infrastructure layer of the AI economy is where the biggest bets are being placed right now. Key Takeaways: Anthropic has received investor offers valuing it at $800 billion, more than double its $350 billion valuation from February 2026. Jane Street committed $6 billion to CoreWeave's AI cloud platform plus a $1 billion equity investment at $109 per share. Broadcom expanded a multi-year partnership with Meta exceeding 1 gigawatt of compute capacity through 2029. Anthropic's Valuation Doubles in Two Months According to Bloomberg, Anthropic has received offers from investors for a new funding round that could bring its valuation to $800 billion. The company raised $30 billion in February at a pre-money valuation of $350 billion, meaning the new figure represents a more-than-doubling in just two months. The surge is driven by Anthropic's run-rate revenue climbing to $30 billion annually, up from $19 billion just months earlier. Strong enterprise adoption of its Claude AI models has made it one of the fastest-growing companies in tech history. Anthropic has not yet accepted these terms and is reportedly considering a potential IPO in Q4 2026. The company's restraint in fundraising suggests management is prioritizing business fundamentals over dilution. CoreWeave and Broadcom Lock In Multi-Billion-Dollar Deals CoreWeave announced that Jane Street has committed approximately $6 billion to use its AI cloud platform, including access to NVIDIA's (NVDA) next-generation Vera Rubin technology. Jane Street also made a $1 billion equity investment in CoreWeave Class A stock at $109 per share, making it the company's fifth-largest shareholder. CoreWeave's Max Hjelm stated that \"Jane Street operates like a frontier lab, continually breaking new ground in deep learning.\" This marks the third multi-billion-dollar deal for the Nvidia-backed cloud company in a single week, following an expanded $21 billion agreement with Meta. Separately, Broadcom announced a multi-year, multi-generation partnership with Meta through 2029 for custom AI accelerator chips. The initial commitment exceeds 1 gigawatt of compute capacity as the first phase of a broader multi-gigawatt rollout. Broadcom shares rose"

Latest news Video Bitcoin DeFi NFT Ethereum Altcoins Blockchain Mining Finance Metaverse Legal Security Analytics Exchange Other GameFi ICO Latest news Video Bitcoin DeFi NFT Ethereum Altcoins Blockchain Mining Finance Metaverse Legal Security Analytics Exchange Other GameFi ICO Back to the list CoreWeave signs $6B deal with Jane Street to provide AI compute power cointelegraph.com 17 h CoreWeave, a publicly traded AI cloud infrastructure company, announced on Wednesday a $6 billion deal with quantitative trading firm Jane Street, in which the firm will use CoreWeave\u2019s AI cloud computing infrastructure to power its trading and research operations. Under the agreement, CoreWeave will provide Jane Street with compute from multiple data center facilities, the company's announcement said. Jane Street also purchased $1 billion in CoreWeave Class A Common stock at $109 per share, according to CoreWeave. Shares of CoreWeave (CRWV) rose by 1.5% on Wednesday, climbing to about $119.04 at the time of publication, according to data from Yahoo Finance. Shares of CRWV rose modestly on Wednesday after the $6 billion Jane Street deal was announced. Source: Yahoo Finance The deal comes about one week after CoreWeave announced an agreement with Anthropic, in which the AI developer would use CoreWeave\u2019s compute infrastructure to power its Claude AI large language models. CoreWeave\u2019s pivot to AI predates the crypto mining industry\u2019s shift by years, and highlights how miners can repurpose their infrastructure to power high-performance computing and shore up declining revenues amid a challenging economic environment. Related: CoreWeave\u2019s $8.5B loan shows how AI is replacing crypto mining finance CoreWeave dominates \u201cneocloud\u201d computing sector CoreWeave was founded as a crypto mining company called Atlantic Crypto, in 2017, before beginning a pivot to AI cloud computing infrastructure in 2019. The company\u2019s shift to AI infrastructure years ahead of the crypto mining industry\u2019s rush into the sector helped establish CoreWeave as a leading \u201cneocloud\u201d company, according to analysts from asset management and investment research company Bernstein. Quarterly revenues of CoreWeave, IREN and Nebius compared. Source: Bernstein \u201cNeocloud\u201d service providers are cloud computing companies built around graphics processing units (GPUs), which power artificial intelligence workloads. Traditional cloud service providers power their operations with basic computer processing units (CPUs) suitable for running websites, Web2 platforms, video games, media streaming and applications. The analysts compared CoreWeave with IREN and Nebius, and concluded that \u201crelative to its neocloud peers, CRWV has by far the strongest commercial machine.\u201d CoreWeave benefits from a mix of contractual agreements and on-demand revenue-generating activities, while also commanding a diverse customer base, Bernstein said. \u201cNine of the leading 10 AI model providers now leverage CoreWeave\u2019s platform,\u201d spokespeople for CoreWeave said following the Anthropic deal in"

It\u2019s the second landmark contract announcement in two consecutive trading days, and the market is taking notice. Thursday brought a $21 billion expanded agreement with Meta Platforms to supply AI cloud capacity through December 2032 . Friday delivers Anthropic. CoreWeave is assembling a who\u2019s-who roster of the world\u2019s most important AI companies, and the pace is accelerating. The intraday price action tells the story clearly. CoreWeave stock opened at $93.44, surged to an intraday high of $105.90 by 11:00 a.m. EST, and has since stabilized near $103 through the afternoon session. The peak volume candle hit at 10:30 a.m. EST, with over 4.4 million shares trading in a single 15-minute window. Anthropic Deal Adds a Crown Jewel to the Customer List Under the multiyear agreement, CoreWeave will provide computing capabilities to build and power Anthropic\u2019s Claude AI models, with Anthropic running workloads at production scale. The agreement starts with a phased rollout and includes the option to expand in the future. Financial terms were not disclosed. The significance of landing Anthropic as a customer is hard to overstate."

IREN , Stock IREN Stock Surges as Key Hardware Delivery Keeps Texas Project on Track 14.04.2026 - 20:35:04 | boerse-global.de IREN shares surged as a critical hardware delivery kept its 1.4GW Texas data center on track for a 2026 launch, fueling its AI cloud pivot backed by a major Microsoft deal. IREN Stock Surges as Key Hardware Delivery Keeps Texas Project on Track - Foto: \u00fcber boerse-global.de Shares of IREN jumped sharply on Tuesday, climbing 8.22% to $46.61, as investors cheered a critical hardware delivery for its flagship data center project. The company received the fourth substation transformer for its Sweetwater 1 facility in Texas, keeping the 1.4-gigawatt development precisely on schedule for an April 2026 launch. The broader AI infrastructure sector has faced pressure recently, but IREN\u2019s momentum appears undeterred. Trading volume on Tuesday more than doubled the daily average, and bullish sentiment is evident in the options market, where the put-call ratio fell to 0.55. The stock is now up roughly 30% for the week and has gained a staggering 820% year-to-date, widening its gap above the 50-day moving average of $36.06. Analyst Consensus Remains Firmly Bullish This optimism is mirrored by Wall Street analysts. More than 70% of covering analysts maintain a bullish stance, with a consensus price target of $80\u2014implying upside potential of about 130% from current levels. As of April 13, eleven analysts have issued buy ratings. While Cantor Fitzgerald trimmed its target from $82 to $61 on April 9, its message stayed constructive, calling AI infrastructure \"an attractive investment area.\" The pullback to the March low of $30 seems to have been digested for now. The company\u2019s strategic pivot from a pure-play Bitcoin miner to a provider of AI cloud services is central to this bullish thesis. A landmark $9.7 billion agreement with Microsoft to supply computing capacity through the end of 2026 underpins this shift. To meet demand, IREN is aggressively scaling its capabilities, having recently finalized the purchase of over 50,000 Nvidia B300 graphics processing units. Its long-term ambition is to grow this fleet to 150,000 units. Should investors sell immediately? Or is it worth buying IREN? Financial Backing and Growth Targets Financing for this expansion looks solid. At the end of 2025, IREN held $3.26 billion in liquid cash. Furthermore, it secured GPU"

    "content": "IREN stock analysis amid CoreWeave, Nebius market share gains \u2014 TradingView News Search Products Community Markets Brokers More EN Get started News / Invezz / IREN stock analysis amid CoreWeave, Nebius market share gains IREN stock analysis amid CoreWeave, Nebius market share gains 2 min read IREN IREN stock price has rebounded in the past few days, moving from a low of $30 on March 30 to the current $40. It remains down by 50% from its highest point in 2025. So, will the stock continue rising as top companies like CoreWeave and Nebius extend their market share? CoreWeave and Nebius are gaining their market share IREN stock price has remained under pressure in the past few weeks and is trailing other popular companies like CoreWeave and Nebius, which are slowly becoming the two biggest players in the industry. For example, CoreWeave has continued to attract customers, including the recent expansion of its deal with Meta Platforms. The company ended the last quarter with a revenue backlog of over $60 billion. Nebius, another NVIDIA-backed company, has continued gaining momentum in the past few months. For example, it recently inked a $27 billion deal with Meta Platforms, one of the biggest deals in the industry. IREN, on the other hand, has not received any major deal with a hyperscaler company this year. To date, its biggest deal was the $9.7 billion partnership with Microsoft, which was announced last year. The stock has also lagged behind its top rivals because of its growing debt load. Its total debt has jumped to over $3.6 billion from $963 million in June last year. The company has continued to boost its debt this year. For example, it raised $2.3 billion in December and has brought in over $9.3 billion in the past eight months through debt, customer prepayments, GPU leasing, and convertible notes. IREN has also announced a $6 billion At-the-Market (ATM) equity offering program to fund the purchase of 150k GPUs. This means that the number of outstanding shares will continue rising, diluting its investors. The other challenge is that other companies continue to enter the AI data center industry. Some of these companies are companies like Riot Platforms, Mara Holdings, and Cipher Mining. These concerns explain why investors have continued to short the company, with the short interest rising to 16%. On the positive side, the company is in a growing industry, with the prices of GPU rentals rising by 40% in the last 60 days. As such, even without large hyperscalers, the company\u2019s growth is expected to keep growing. Analysts expect the upcoming report to show that its revenue rose by 48% in the last quarter to $219 million. For the year, the revenue is expected to jump to $1.01 billion and $2.09 billion this year and the next one. These numbers will represent a 97% YoY and 193% YoY growth. Search Blog Markets Insights Apr 15, 2026 AI Data Center Investments Face Delays, IREN Secures Key Advantages According to a recent report from Yahoo Finance , substantial capital is being directed toward the construction of artificial intelligence data centers. Amazon and Alphabet are among the firms making large-scale commitments for such facilities in Mississippi and Texas , respectively. Despite this influx of investment, a significant portion of data center projects across the United States have encountered delays or cancellations. These setbacks are attributed to supply chain constraints and dependencies on imported components. Such challenges increase the value of existing operational data centers and provide an advantage to companies that began their infrastructure development earlier. IREN Secures Energy and Chips for AI Operations IREN has positioned itself by securing a substantial amount of renewable energy capacity for its data centers, exceeding 4.5 gigawatts following an acquisition in Oklahoma. Energy access is a critical constraint, with grid interconnection processes often taking years. The company currently operates data centers with a capacity of 810 megawatts and expects to energize a major new site imminently. IREN has contracted annual recurring revenue of $2.3 billion, anchored by a multi-year agreement with Microsoft for 200 megawatts of capacity. IREN differentiates its business model by purchasing advanced chips directly for its data centers, which increases its value per megawatt compared to firms that require clients to supply their own hardware. The company recently expanded its fleet of graphics processing units with a large new order. 1. Aditya Raghunath \u2022 7 minute read Reviewed by: Thomas Richmond Last updated Apr 10, 2026 Key Takeaways: Nebius is forecast to generate $15.23 billion in revenue by 2028, with gross margins expanding toward 95% \u2014 a profile that increasingly resembles a software company rather than a hardware landlord. Iren has secured a $9.7 billion Microsoft contract and targets $3.4 billion in annualized run-rate revenue by the end of 2026, backed by over 4.5 gigawatts of secured power \u2014 but its margin profile remains thinner than Nebius. Both stocks have pulled back sharply from their 2025 highs. Wall Street\u2019s rapidly expanding analyst coverage of both names suggests institutional conviction is building, though Nebius commands a higher valuation multiple and a stronger margin outlook. Now Live: Discover how much upside your favorite stocks could have using TIKR\u2019s new Valuation Model (It\u2019s free) >>> Two artificial intelligence infrastructure plays are grabbing investor attention right now. Both are building the data centers that power the AI boom. Both are early-stage, burning cash, and priced on future potential rather than today\u2019s profits. But Wall Street doesn\u2019t treat them equally \u2014 and the gap is wider than you might think. Nebius Group ( NBIS ) and Iren Limited ( IREN ) are chasing the same tailwind: surging demand for GPU compute. The question is which one has stronger analyst conviction. The numbers tell a clear story. Nebius is the Amsterdam-listed rebuild of Yandex\u2019s core AI and cloud infrastructure business. Today, Nebius operates a full-stack AI cloud \u2014 think GPU clusters, managed storage, and developer tools \u2014 targeting AI start-ups and enterprises that need compute but can\u2019t afford to build their own. At a Morgan Stanley conference in March 2026, Volozh described the ambition plainly: the company is targeting nearly one gigawatt of active power this year and has a pipeline reserved for up to three gigawatts. \u201cDemand far exceeds our supply,\u201d Nebius Chief Revenue Officer Marc Boroditsky told the audience, according to the conference transcript. Iren is an Australian-founded company that spent seven years as a Bitcoin miner before pivoting hard into AI cloud hosting. It now operates 810 megawatts of existing data centers \u2014 predominantly air-cooled, which is increasingly in demand \u2014 and has secured a $9.7 billion AI contract with Microsoft. IREN is up 715% year over year, but has taken a 38% haircut off its Nov. 5 all-time high of $76.87. This recent rally could have staying power, with IREN clearing a historically bullish trendline. The trendline in question is the stock's 50-day moving average. Per Schaeffer's Senior Quantitative Analyst Rocky White, this \u201ccrossover\u201d event happened six other times over the last 10 years, after which IREN was higher one month later at least half of the time, averaging a whopping 33.7% gain. From its current perch, a move higher of this magnitude would put the shares at their highest level of 2026. Although calls outnumber puts on an absolute basis, bears have been circling the former blockchain maven. IREN sports a 50-day put/call volume ratio at the International Securities Exchange (ISE), Chicago Board Options Exchange (CBOE), and NASDAQ OMX PHLX (PHLX) that sits higher than 99% of annual readings. Experiences Valuation Adjustment Amidst Shifts in Financial Metrics and Performance IREN Ltd. Experiences Valuation Adjustment Amidst Shifts in Financial Metrics and Performance 4 hours ago share Share Via IREN Ltd., a midcap in the Commercial Services & Supplies sector, has experienced a valuation adjustment, with a high P/E ratio of 246 and a price-to-book value of 8.92. Despite a negative ROCE and modest ROE, the company has achieved impressive stock returns, significantly outperforming the S&P 500. Get Detailed Stock Report --> IREN Ltd., a midcap player in the Commercial Services & Supplies sector, has recently undergone a valuation adjustment, reflecting shifts in its financial metrics. The company's price-to-earnings (P/E) ratio stands at 246, indicating a significant premium compared to typical industry benchmarks. Additionally, IREN's price-to-book value is reported at 8.92, while its enterprise value to EBITDA ratio is noted at 123.01, suggesting a complex financial landscape. The company's return on capital employed (ROCE) is currently at -3.38%, and its return on equity (ROE) is at 3.63%, which may raise questions about profitability relative to its equity base. Baillie Gifford trims its IREN stake in Q4 Scotland-based asset manager Baillie Gifford disclosed in a filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission that it had reduced its IREN position by 6.3% during the fourth quarter of 2025, selling 35,307 shares. The firm now holds 527,686 shares, representing roughly 0.19% of the company, valued at approximately $19.93 million. Not all major investors moved in the same direction. Invesco Ltd. grew its IREN holdings by 45.9% during the second quarter, reaching 2,974,674 shares valued at $43.34 million, while NewEdge Advisors expanded its position by 245.0% over the same period. Sei Investments and StoneX Group each established new positions, and Peapack Gladstone Financial Corp increased its stake by 30.0%. In the fourth quarter, Wells Fargo and Jane Street Group significantly reduced their holdings, while Marex Group and BNP Paribas moved in the opposite direction. Institutional investors now hold 41.08% of the company\u2019s outstanding shares. A quarterly earnings report that missed on both counts IREN\u2019s most recent earnings report, issued February 5, fell short of analyst expectations on both key measures. The company reported a loss of $0.44 per share for the quarter, well below the $0.07 per share loss analysts had projected. Revenue came in at $184.69 million, missing the consensus estimate of $229.64 million and reflecting a year-over-year decline of 23.1%. Despite the miss, Wall Street analysts hold a Moderate Buy consensus on the stock with an average price target of $70.08. IREN shares opened at $43.07 today, well below the 52-week high of $76.87 but substantially above the 52-week low of $5.24. Fierce competition and a growing debt load add pressure IREN operates in a rapidly crowding field. Rivals CoreWeave and Nebius , both backed by NVIDIA , have accelerated their market positions. Nebius recently secured a $27 billion deal with Meta Platforms, while CoreWeave ended its latest quarter with a revenue backlog exceeding $60 billion. IREN\u2019s largest active partnership remains a $9.7 billion deal with Microsoft announced last year, and the company has yet to land a comparable hyperscaler contract in 2026. Debt has climbed steeply, rising to $3.6 billion from $963 million in June 2025, driven by capital expenditure on hardware.     "content": "Why IREN&#8217;s Massive AI Cloud Transformation Could Trigger a $608 Target | TIKR.com Product Pricing Company Resources Blog Sign In Free Sign Up Free Sign Up EN General Investing Why IREN\u2019s Massive AI Cloud Transformation Could Trigger a $608 Target Wiltone Asuncion \u2022 7 minute read Reviewed by: David Hanson Last updated Mar 27, 2026 K ey Stats for IREN Stock Current Price: $42 Target Price: $608 Street Target: $76.14 Annualized Return: 86.7% / year Now Live: Discover how much upside your favorite stocks could have using TIKR\u2019s new Valuation Model (It\u2019s free) >>> What Happened? The artificial intelligence arms race has shifted from silicon to electricity. The constraint is no longer just buying Nvidia chips; it is finding the grid-connected real estate that can actually power them. IREN Limited ( IREN ) is proving it has solved this infrastructure choke point. During the company\u2019s Q2 2026 Earnings Call on February 5, Co-CEO Daniel Roberts detailed how the former Bitcoin miner is successfully executing a total pivot to become a vertically integrated AI cloud platform. On the capacity side, IREN announced the procurement of a massive new 1.6-gigawatt (GW) site in Oklahoma, pushing its total secured power pipeline to over 4.5 GW. On the capital front, IREN effectively de-risked its immediate future by securing a $3.6 billion financing package from Goldman Sachs and JPMorgan. Combined with $1.9 billion in upfront cash prepayments, IREN fully funded 95% of the capital expenditures required for its colossal $9.7 billion AI contract with Microsoft. \u201cWe have secured more than 4.5 gigawatts of power, stood up 810 megawatts of operating data centers, signed billions of dollars of AI customer contracts and assembled a team of over 2,000 people to execute on them,\u201d Roberts noted on the call, emphasizing a physical moat that newcomers cannot easily replicate. Despite this operational triumph, the market\u2019s initial Earnings Reaction was a modest +5.13% gain on the day of the report. However, management remains entirely focused on long-term scale. Following the earnings call, IREN announced on March 4, 2026, that it entered into purchase agreements for over 50,000 NVIDIA B300 GPUs . This massive procurement expands their total fleet beyond the transcript\u2019s 140,000 target, bringing the new fleet size to an incredible 150,000 GPUs and upgrading their targeted Annualized Run Rate (ARR) to over $3.7 billion by the end of 2026. IREN Stock Price Target (TIKR) See historical and forward estimates for IREN stock (It\u2019s free!) >>> Is IREN Undervalued Today? Evaluating IREN requires comparing its business model against other Bitcoin miners pivoting into AI, such as Core Scientific (CORZ), TeraWulf (WULF), and Hut 8 (HUT). Many of these peers are pursuing a \u201ccolocation\u201d strategy. In a colocation model, the mining company simply builds the data center shell, secures the power, and rents the physical space to a major tech company. It functions much like a traditional commercial real estate lease. For example, TeraWulf recently acquired 1.5 GW of power across Maryland and Kentucky in February to expand this exact"

    "content": "Exclusive: TD Synnex Nabs Over 1,000 Nvidia GPUs For Channel In Nebius Cloud Deal Exclusive: TD Synnex Nabs Over 1,000 Nvidia GPUs For Channel In Nebius Cloud Deal TD Synnex officials Francisco Criado and Jack Donato tell CRN that the Nebius partnership will help channel partners gain access to high-end GPUs in the cloud, which have become a scarce resource due to high demand from AI-native companies. TD Synnex has reserved over 1,000 high-end Nvidia GPUs in the cloud to help solution providers overcome supply constraints and get their customers moving on AI projects. The U.S. distribution giant, which won Nvidia\u2019s Distribution Partner of the Year award for the Americas last month , made the cloud commitment for AI computing clusters based on Nvidia\u2019s B300 GPU with neocloud provider Nebius. TD Synnex announced the deal on Tuesday at its High-Growth Conference in Puerto Rico. [Related: Nvidia Channel Chief: Neoclouds Represent \u2018Next Phase Of Opportunity\u2019 For Partners ] The Fremont, Calif.-based company is accepting purchase orders for B300-based instances through Nebius now, with availability expected to begin in July. The agreement lasts for a year, and it gives TD Synnex the option for renewal. Nvidia Americas channel chief Craig Weinstein told CRN last month that the company sees the partnership as an important way to reach enterprise customers. \u201cIt means that we have an [Nvidia Cloud Partner] that\u2019s going to offer cloud capability to the ecosystem to ensure [that] enterprises can iterate and solution providers have a platform to leverage,\u201d he said. TD Synnex-Nebius Deal Seen As Way To Combat Supply Scarcity In an exclusive interview with CRN, TD Synnex officials Francisco Criado and Jack Donato said that the Nebius partnership will help channel partners gain access to what has become a scarce resource due to high demand from AI-native companies. \u201cThere\u2019s literally no capacity available right now,\u201d said Criado, senior vice president of cloud, security and AI at TD Synnex. TD Synnex expects the Nebius deal to benefit many of its strategic partners who otherwise \u201cwouldn\u2019t get the prioritization that they would need to gain access to the GPUs,\u201d according to Donato, AI and neocloud vendor solutions at TD Synnex. \u201cOur ability to give them that prioritization up there with the general hyperscalers and neocloud providers just gives them access, in general,\u201d he said. \u201cCould they get the same capacity direct? Maybe, but there\u2019s no availability.\u201d TD Synnex: Deal Represents First Of Its Kind In Disty World According to TD Synnex, the Nebius engagement \u201cmarks the first time a global IT distributor has reserved a dedicated Nvidia AI factory-grade cluster from an AI-native cloud provider.\u201d \u201cIt\u2019s not your typical distribution go-to-market where you\u2019re buying and reselling something,\u201d Criado said. \u201cWe\u2019re actually committing and we\u2019ve purchased this reserved instance within the Nebius data center that we own, and we will now work with our partners and their customers to [drive consumption].\u201d Based on Nvidia\u2019s Blackwell Ultra architecture, the B300 debuted last year as the company\u2019s flagship GPU for x86-based servers that"

The share price move has been sharp, with returns of 37.3% over the past week, 36.8% over the last 30 days, 71.8% year to date and a very large gain over the past year that is around 7x. Recent attention on Nebius Group has centered on its role in the software space and how investors are treating high growth names in the current market. This broader interest helps explain why the stock has attracted fresh capital and sharper swings in sentiment. Nebius Group currently has a valuation score of 3 out of 6 . The rest of this article will compare different valuation approaches for the stock and then finish with a way to think about value that goes beyond any single model. Nebius Group delivered 645.6% returns over the last year. See how this stacks up to the rest of the Software industry. Advertisement Approach 1: Nebius Group Discounted Cash Flow (DCF) Analysis A Discounted Cash Flow, or DCF, model takes projected future cash flows and discounts them back to what they could be worth today using a required rate of return. It is a way of translating long term cash flow expectations into a single present value per share. For Nebius Group, the model uses a 2 Stage Free Cash Flow to Equity approach based on cash flow projections in US$. The latest twelve month free cash flow is a loss of $3,545.57 million. Analyst estimates and extrapolations then feed into ten year projections that move from further free cash flow losses in the near term to a projected free cash flow of $4,780 million in 2030, with additional extrapolated figures through 2035. Bringing all of those projected cash flows back to today gives an estimated intrinsic value of US$627.77 per share. Compared to the current share price of US$154.56, the DCF output suggests that Nebius Group is about 75.4% undervalued on this model. Result: UNDERVALUED Our Discounted Cash Flow (DCF) analysis suggests Nebius Group is undervalued by 75.4%. The buildout is real, execution is real, and the opportunity appears to dwarf what the market has priced in. Reason 1: The Revenue Trajectory Is Unlike Anything Seen in the Sector When a company posts $227.7 million in Q4 2025 revenue, up 503.6% year-over-year, analysts pay attention. When it exits the year with an annualized run-rate of $1.25 billion and guides for $7 billion to $9 billion ARR by end of 2026, it warrants a closer look. Full-year 2025 revenue came in at $529.8 million, up 350.89% year-over-year. The AI cloud segment alone generated $214.2 million in Q4, representing 94% of total group revenue. CEO Arkady Volozh said it plainly on the Q4 earnings call: \u201cEverything we build, we sell.\u201d Reason 2: The EBITDA Inflection Tells Us the Business Model Works Adjusted EBITDA strips out aggressive infrastructure depreciation and shows whether the core business is economically viable, a more relevant lens given Nebius\u2019s heavy investment phase. In Q4 2025, group adjusted EBITDA turned positive for the first time at $15 million, compared to a loss of $63.9 million a year earlier. The AI cloud segment reached a 24% adjusted EBITDA margin in Q4, up from 19% in Q3. Operating leverage is visible: cost of revenue declined from 60% to 30% of revenue year-over-year in Q4 2025. Lifestyle Leadership \u2709\ufe0f Emails \ud83c\udfa7 Podcasts Untitled Nebius Pipeline Growth Supports Strong 2026 Revenue Outlook NBIS' AI cloud boom pushes pipeline toward $4B, with longer contracts, tight capacity and strong demand shaping its 2026 outlook. Originally published on Zacks Investment Research Share to X Share to Facebook Share to Reddit Share to Email Share to Link Share to X Share to Facebook Share to Reddit Share to Email Share to Link Add Quartz on Google Nebius Group N.V. NBIS is witnessing strong momentum in its business pipeline, reinforcing confidence in its growth outlook for 2026 as demand for AI cloud services continues to accelerate. On the last earnings call, the company reported that its pipeline is expanding significantly, supported by sustained customer interest and increasing adoption across both AI-native companies and enterprise clients. This robust demand environment has led to consistent capacity sellouts, including the latter half of 2025 and into early 2026, highlighting a continued imbalance between supply and demand. Pipeline expansion is being supported by stronger customer commitments and sustained growth across both new and existing accounts. The company noted a significant increase in contract durations, with new agreements extending by nearly 50% in the fourth quarter of 2025, reflecting a shift toward longer-term engagements. Pricing trends have remained stable across GPU offerings, including older-generation units, supported by consistently high utilization. At the same time, AI-native firms are rapidly scaling their operations, evolving into enterprise-level customers with increasing compute needs, while established enterprises continue to embed AI deeper into their core business processes. Related Content Boeing is hiring 100 to 140 factory workers a week to boost production Ram is recalling 65,000 trucks over a software glitch that can freeze dashboard displays Improving deal dynamics are contributing to the strength of the pipeline. There is also a growing trend of customers securing capacity in advance, including through prepayments, to ensure access amid tight supply conditions. Reflecting this continued momentum, the company indicated that its pipeline creation in early 2026 is on track to surpass $4 billion, supported by steady demand and ongoing sales execution. Backed by these trends, Nebius reiterated its confidence in its 2026 targets. The company maintained its annualized run-rate revenue (ARR) guidance of $7 billion to $9 billion for the year. The company accelerated its capacity plans, announcing nine new data centers and securing more than 2 gigawatts of contracted power, with expectations to exceed 3 gigawatts. Nebius remains on track to deliver 800 megawatts to 1 gigawatt of available data center capacity by year-end 2026. With a strong pipeline, expanding capacity and continued demand, Nebius remains well-positioned to deliver on its growth plans. For 2026, the company expects revenue to be between $3 billion and $3.4 billion. The company anticipates the group adjusted EBITDA margin to be approximately 40%. For now, this news mainly reinforces, rather than changes, that central risk reward trade off. The Lappeenranta plan sits alongside Nebius\u2019s US$27 billion, five year AI infrastructure agreement with Meta, which is currently the most important commercial proof point behind its capacity build. While the Meta deal anchors demand for future sites, including Finland, it also raises concentration risk and heightens execution pressure: Nebius has committed to deliver substantial capacity from 2027, and delays or cost overruns at new campuses could directly affect that flagship contract. Search Blog Markets Insights Apr 13, 2026 Nebius Group: AI Infrastructure Powerhouse Sees Stock Surge and Major Contracts Nebius Group , trading on the NASDAQ, has emerged as a neocloud company specializing in constructing data centers designed for artificial intelligence workloads. According to a report from Yahoo Finance , the company was formed after the former Yandex N.V. divested its Russian business assets to concentrate on AI infrastructure. The company's stock has seen a significant increase in value since the start of the prior year. This surge in investor interest followed financial backing from Nvidia and the securing of substantial contracts. Analysts have shown optimism, with a median price target indicating potential for further growth from its current trading level. Role in AI Infrastructure Nebius builds specialized, GPU-powered data centers, sometimes termed AI factories, to address computing resource constraints faced by AI developers and hyperscalers. This model allows client companies to expand their capacity more rapidly than by constructing their own facilities. The company has secured multiyear agreements with Microsoft and Meta Platforms . The total value of these contracts is reported to be substantial, demonstrating significant demand from major technology firms. Financial Prospects and Strategic Moves These major contracts are expected to drive considerable revenue growth for Nebius. The company concluded the previous year with reported total revenue in the hundreds of millions. Analyst projections indicate a sharp increase for the current year and an even larger figure for the following year. Nvidia's multi-billion dollar investment in the company is viewed as a strategic advantage, potentially granting Nebius earlier access to future hardware platforms. Furthermore, Nebius recently moved to acquire an agentic AI search company. This acquisition, valued in the hundreds of millions, is seen as an effort to strengthen its position within a specific and promising segment of the AI market, which research firms estimate could grow significantly over the next ten years. 1.     "content": "MLPerf\u00ae Inference v6.0: Top-tier AI performance on NVIDIA Blackwell and Blackwell Ultra Search Contact sales Log in to Token Factory Log in to AI Cloud AI Cloud Token Factory Solutions Why Nebius Pricing Docs Resources Company For investors Blog / Platform news / MLPerf\u00ae Inference v6.0: Top-tier AI performance on NVIDIA Blackwell and Blackwell Ultra April 1, 2026 14 mins to read Share Today we\u2019re announcing our latest submission to the MLPerf\u00ae Inference v6.0 benchmark suite, the industry-standard benchmark for measuring AI inference performance developed by MLCommons\u00ae. In this round, Nebius continued to demonstrate strong results for AI inference workloads running on the latest NVIDIA platforms. Our submission includes systems built on NVIDIA Blackwell and Blackwell Ultra GPUs \u2014 NVIDIA HGX B200, NVIDIA HGX B300 and the rack-scale NVIDIA GB300 NVL72 system. We benchmarked three models in this round: DeepSeek R1, Qwen3-VL 235B and gpt-oss 120B. Two of these models, Qwen3-VL 235B, a multimodal model capable of understanding both visual and text inputs; and gpt-oss 120B, a large open-source language model, were introduced to the MLPerf\u00ae Inference benchmark suite in this release. Together with DeepSeek R1, these benchmarks represent the type of frontier-scale models increasingly used in modern AI applications. The MLPerf\u00ae benchmark suite continues to evolve alongside the AI industry, introducing new workloads that reflect the latest trends in large language and multimodal models. Our results in this round demonstrate Nebius\u2019 readiness to run these frontier-scale workloads on cutting-edge AI infrastructure. Ready for frontier model inference Ready for frontier model inference In the MLPerf\u00ae Inference v6.0 round, Nebius benchmarked three NVIDIA AI systems built on the latest Blackwell and Blackwell Ultra architectures: NVIDIA HGX B200 NVIDIA HGX B300 NVIDIA GB300 NVL72 Overall, Nebius achieved 10 first-place results out of 16 benchmark submissions, along with other vendors submitting results. We also submitted five additional use cases where we were the only submitter, so we do not count them as first-place results.     "content": "Nebius Talks With AI21 Labs Test Full Stack AI Ambitions - Simply Wall St News Home Portfolios Watchlist Community Discover Screener Sign Up or Login Home Portfolios Watchlist Community Discover Screener Advertisement United States / Software / NasdaqGS:NBIS Nebius Talks With AI21 Labs Test Full Stack AI Ambitions April 11, 2026 Simply Wall St Reviewed by Bailey Pemberton Nebius Group, ticker NasdaqGS:NBIS, is reportedly in advanced talks to acquire Israeli AI startup AI21 Labs. The potential deal would mark a move beyond Nebius\u2019s current focus on AI infrastructure into proprietary AI software and platform offerings. This M&A development has not featured in recent coverage that has centered on Nebius\u2019s data center plans, analyst attention, and valuation scenarios. Nebius Group enters these reported talks with AI21 Labs after a period of very strong share price performance, with NasdaqGS:NBIS up 33.2% over the past week and 29.4% over the past month. The stock is also up 61.2% year to date and has delivered a very large return over the past year from its current share price of $144.97. That backdrop means any confirmed move into AI applications is likely to be closely watched by investors already focused on Nebius\u2019s growth story. If Nebius proceeds with acquiring AI21 Labs, it would extend the business from infrastructure into higher margin AI software and platforms, broadening its addressable market. For you as an investor, key questions include how Nebius integrates AI21\u2019s technology, what this means for future spending, and whether a fuller AI stack changes how the market values NasdaqGS:NBIS over time. Stay updated on the most important news stories for Nebius Group by adding it to your watchlist or portfolio . Alternatively, explore our Community to discover new perspectives on Nebius Group. NasdaqGS:NBIS Earnings & Revenue Growth as at Apr 2026 We've flagged 4 risks for Nebius Group. See which could impact your investment. For Nebius, moving from selling AI infrastructure into owning language models and agent software would push it closer to the full-stack approach used by providers such as Amazon, Microsoft and Google. AI21 Labs brings large language models and its Maestro agent management product, which could sit on top of Nebius data centers and help convert contracted GPU capacity into higher-margin software and platform revenue. At the same time, the rumored purchase price of several billion dollars would add to an already heavy capital program linked to Meta and Microsoft contracts, so investors need to weigh the extra software upside against balance sheet and integration demands. AI21\u2019s earlier struggle to gain broad traction with its models also raises a question about commercial execution and how Nebius might position these products against better-known rivals. Advertisement How This Fits Into The Nebius Group Narrative The talks with AI21 Labs align with the narrative that Nebius wants to build a full-stack AI platform on top of its large data center build-out and long-term contracts with Meta and Microsoft. Nebius unveiled plans on Tuesday to build a new AI data center in Finland. The new facility will be one of Europe's largest when completed, the company said. In this article NBIS Follow your favorite stocks CREATE FREE ACCOUNT Nebius unveiled plans on Tuesday to build a new AI data center in Finland, which it says will be one of Europe's largest when up-and-running, as the region races to develop the compute infrastructure needed to power the AI boom. The new facility will be based in the Finnish city of Lappeenranta with a capacity of up to 310 MW. Nebius said it expects the data center to begin initially supplying customers by 2027. \"We have been building in Finland for many years and are pleased to be expanding our presence here,\" said Nebius CEO Arkady Volozh in a statement. \"Lappeenranta represents a significant addition to our global AI infrastructure build-out, and will make a significant contribution to achieving our capacity goals.\" Europe's AI buildout It comes amid a slew of AI infrastructure announcements across Europe. French AI startup Mistral said Monday it had secured $830 million in debt financing to operate a data center near Paris. That came after a February announcement of a 1.2-billion-euro ($1.38 billion) plan to build data centers and compute capacity in Sweden. U.K. startup Nscale announced it had raised $2 billion at a $14.6 billion valuation earlier this month, with plans to develop AI data centers in its home market alongside Europe and the U.S. Meanwhile, 2025 saw MGX, Bpifrance, Mistral and Nvidia unveil plans for a 1.4 GW AI campus in France and Brookfield said it"

CTech 09:26, 09.04.26 AI21 Labs is in acquisition talks once again, this time with AI infrastructure company Nebius, according to a report by The Information, months after earlier negotiations with Nvidia failed to result in a deal. Calcalist previously reported that the Israeli startup had held advanced discussions with Nvidia at a reported valuation of $2 billion to $3 billion, above its last known valuation of $1.4 billion from 2023. Those talks reached senior levels but ultimately did not materialize into a transaction. 1 View gallery AI21 Labs founders. ( Photo: Orel Cohen ) In January, co-founder and chairman Amnon Shashua confirmed that AI21 was engaged in discussions with Nvidia as well as other potential buyers, indicating that the company was formally exploring strategic options. Founded in 2017 by Shashua, Professor Yoav Shoham and Ori Goshen, AI21 was among the early companies developing large-scale language models. However, it has struggled in recent years to keep pace with leading AI developers such as OpenAI and Anthropic. The company has since narrowed its focus to enterprise applications, emphasizing accuracy and reliability. It discontinued development of its consumer product, Wordtune, and now centers on offerings such as its Maestro platform and a recently introduced reasoning model. Annual revenue is estimated at around $50 million. Related articles: \u201cThere are talks with Nvidia and others\u201d: Shashua confirms AI21 acquisition discussions AI21 never closed its reported $300 million round as Nvidia weighs an acquisition Nvidia in advanced talks to acquire AI21 in $2-3 billion deal focused on talent AI21 employs roughly 200 people, down from about 260 in 2023, after scaling back expansion plans. Much of the interest in the company has been attributed to its workforce, which includes highly trained AI researchers. The earlier Nvidia talks were widely viewed as driven primarily by this talent base, rather than by AI21\u2019s commercial"

Nebius posted 500% YoY quarterly growth with $529.8M trailing 12-month revenue and $3.68B cash on hand, positioning it to fund both the AI21 acquisition and its $16-20B data center capex plan. Model-development costs climbing into the billions are driving consolidation among AI infrastructure players, with Nebius seeking to build a full-stack platform after Nvidia walked away from AI21. Investors who own the stock or watch the AI-cloud space want one clear answer: does this move turn Nebius into a full-stack contender, or is it just another high-priced gamble in a sector already priced for perfection? The Rumored Deal\u2019s Details The Information reported Wednesday that Nebius entered talks to buy AI21 Labs, months after Nvidia\u2019s negotiations collapsed. Founded in 2017, AI21 once ranked among the early leaders in large language models. It has since pivoted to enterprise tools such as the Maestro platform and a new reasoning model after discontinuing its consumer product Wordtune. Annual revenue sits at roughly $50 million. Nebius is one of the more unusual players in the AI boom: a \"startup\" that didn't exactly start from scratch. Loading audio narration... The company emerged in 2024 from the breakup of Russian search giant Yandex . A complex transaction left the new Nebius with hundreds of employees and roughly $2.5 billion in capital, giving it a scale and war chest most new entrants can only dream of. With decades of experience building massive data centers for a huge online search business, the Nebius team brought deep know-how to the AI infrastructure race. The company builds AI data centers from the ground up. I met Nebius CEO Arkady Volozh when he was in Silicon Valley for Nvidia's GTC conference earlier this month. Search Blog Markets Insights Array 3 High-Growth Stocks for Investors in 2026: IonQ, SoundHound AI, Nebius Loading Stock video by doktorkleinmusic via Pixabay Apr 14, 2026 3 High-Growth Stocks for Investors in 2026: IonQ, SoundHound AI, Nebius Investors seeking significant growth potential are focusing on smaller companies, though such opportunities carry considerable risk. Three stocks are noted for their possible strong returns, though success is not assured. IonQ Quantum computing is developing as a field, with IonQ positioned as a leader due to its current status in accuracy. The company reports substantial demand for its early-stage products and has formed multiple client research partnerships. Its revenue reportedly increased significantly in the final quarter of 2025 compared to the prior year, with expectations for it to double in the following year. The quantum computing sector remains highly competitive. SoundHound AI SoundHound AI develops software that integrates generative artificial intelligence with audio recognition. Its current primary application is in restaurant drive-thru automation and in-vehicle digital assistants. The potential market for this technology is viewed as extensive, with possible future applications in any phone-based human interaction, including customer service areas in healthcare, insurance, and finance. Nebius A third company, Nebius, is also identified as having potential for strong returns in the coming years. 1.
